## 7. Style moments (proposed)

Each needs the [style-range](../../bible/style-range.md) pass's call; this list now matches that pass's Ep3 map (§6.3) item for item. The frequent tier is already in the script: the 2-TONE freeze (F3.1), EARLY-WEB16 (F3.2), masked GLYPH ×2 (the shutter's slats, 12 frames, 3.C; the clock face, 5 frames), LEDGER ×2 (the `$43.4M`, 3.E; the check), and the lit UI.

| Sc | Moment | Tier | Motivation | Build |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 8 | **The sports package:** FAN CAM lower third and a telestrator circle drawn on Mas's head (style-range 3.B ★) | Treatment (P4 SPORTS, a pass at leap weight) | The stadium's cameras try to read the one thinking nobody has been shown | Code |
| 10 | **The strawberry photo:** his lock-screen wallpaper, near-photoreal inside the phone's bezel, under PP3.1's opaque banner (style-range 3.A micro) | Drastic, in a bezel (≈ 2 s) | He posted a real photograph, and the phone keeps it: the only photograph in the dark room, and the plant for J5. It rides a shot already running, and he turns it face down | three.js PBR still now; a Blender still for the final. A generic strawberry, never the real photo or its garden (X1) |
| 19 | **Paper pop-up book** (style-range 3.D ★) | Drastic (tier 2; the band retracts). The episode's one full-frame leap | Misanthropic's founders tell their own origin in their handmade house style | 2.5D hinged planes now; scanned card stock for the final |
| 22 | **STREAM:** `LIVE` bug, viewer count, the calendar as the stream's UI (style-range 3.F) | Treatment (P5 STREAM) | The real twelve days were twelve livestreams | Code. While it's up the show's rail goes dark and the dates ride the stream's lower third, so the pass replaces a layer instead of adding one |

- **Withdrawn: extra blocky on the backward odometer** (Draft 3's sc 15). Extra blocky is THE WHALE's medium in the style-range map (BRANDS: "cheap is the product"), and its first use is Ep4's pebble (4.A), one episode later. Spending it on Mas's odometer would pre-empt the whale and blur what the medium means. The odometer's fall keeps EARLY-WEB16 and LEDGER.
- **Kept against the continuity critic's note to drop STREAM:** the critic's worry was a third interface layer on a climax already built as an interface. In this draft the advent calendar *is* the stream's UI, and the rail goes dark while the pass is up, so SHIPMAS carries one interface, not three. The style-range pass has the final call.
- **Declined, as style-range §5.4 rules:** a style leap at the podium's turn.
- **Drastic changes here:** one full-frame leap (3.D) and one bezel micro (3.A). Ep4's pebble (4.A, extra blocky) is the next.

## 8. The tension sheet

| Movement | The clock | The irony | The turn | The act-out |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Cold open | The yarn racing to the spire | We haven't seen the post; the intro shows it | The city's conspiracy lands on one strawberry | "it's a strawberry." · the chip voice's F · SMASH TO INTRO |
| 1 | o1's `THINKING…`; the countdown clock | THE PLAN's blank shutter slammed on `SHOW THE THINKING.`, so we know one is coming | The thinking goes behind a shutter, and Mas pockets the summary | The man who wants a number writes one the clock can't find · "roughly." · the clack (a diegetic out) |
| 2 | The door's speed; the odometer's wheels | We saw the door go in before anyone left | People out, money in: the price lands, and the owner is "the nonprofit." | `…001` · THREAT |
| 3 | The podium's climb; the bridge's missing planks | The podium has faced away all year, and we've watched it climb | The rival finishes its bridge and hires; the podium reaches the top | The pen's click · THE RUN stops mid-phrase · DREAD |
| 4 | Twelve doors; the line to the podium | We watched him write the check | His coin gets a clink; the bigger number turns the podium | Button: "Say a number." · `verified: president-elect` · brass, no third |

The outs rotate (diegetic, THREAT, a stop plus DREAD, the brass), and none repeats Ep2's last ("the cue resolves").

## 9. Budgets and guardrails, checked

**Budgets (guides, not gates):**
- **Scenes that end on Mas's line:** 1 of 22 (sc 3). 3.4 ends sc 15 on the investors' blank faces instead of "that one had confetti.". The stadium ends on Maon's line and the pocketed note. Where he has the last word but the scene ends on the world (the clock's clack in sc 10, the extra dollar in sc 14, the doorway's light in sc 16–17), the button is the world's.
- **"super.":** none. The word's spine is Ep1 → Ep9 → Ep11; Ep2 and Ep3 no longer spend it as a throwaway.
- **Catchphrases, one clean use each:**
  - "That's a sub-concern." (3.5, restored at door 12, to Adelina, as his scroll goes up on the bar): the season's one callback on Ep1 sc 13's "It has sub-concerns.", which Ep1 holds back in its own sc 17 for this (Ep1 open question 51). 3.3 had cut it on the belief that Ep1's handoff already counted it as cut; Ep1's second pass corrected that, so the Ep1 owner should now mark question 51 settled: Ep3 keeps it.
  - "it's a preview." (its one use here; a callback to Ep1, which uses it four times)
  - "Thank you all." (one clip, twice, the same drawing both times)
  - **Runners (3.4, 3.5):** "how long?" (sc 4, 8; sc 21, where Gerg answers it with a number, 3.5; sc 22, where Maon answers it with "We summarized it."; then Nole's "How long have you been standing there?" in sc 23) and "a while" (Gerg's non-number in sc 4, Maon's "It thought for a while." in sc 22, Mas's own in sc 23; 3.5 keeps Lunchmas's sc 18 line off the word). Each use is a different move, so the runner turns (R10). "Eleven" (3.5, a quiet rhyme nobody points at): Gerg's "I've dropped eleven since August." (sc 17A), then the eleven older pairs of footprints and `ELEVEN LEAVE` (sc 18–19), keys popping off the board like people. The photo: "the class photo can stay up" → the hole → "a smaller photo" → "everybody fit at one table" → "Did you ever get the smaller photo?" / "Good." → Gerg's nod.
  - KOOC's "We're not first. We're best." (3.2; its first use, from his character file)
  - "Addendum:" and "In plain English:" rest this episode.
- **Interruptions (3.2):** no dashes and no designed overlaps. The one restart left, the Second Truther's "just… thinking?", is one speaker changing course, not a cut-off. (3.6 finishes Mario's abandoned "next year it's… I'm drafting something." as a whole thought, because a stock voice reads the dots as a dead stop.) Nobody is cut off anywhere in the episode, because no scene is heated enough to earn it; SHIPMAS's crowd is the one place a burst of crosstalk could go.
- **Talk (3.6, counted by the Ep3 counting script, the same parser as 3.4's and 3.5's, so 3.5's figures in brackets compare directly; older drafts were counted by other parsers, and their figures are in their own logs: 3.2 in §12, 3.3 in §13, 3.4 in §14):** 174 voiced turns and 1,401 words (3.5: 174 and 1,364); a median of 6 words a turn, and 8 for turns by anyone but Mas (3.5: 7); 46 turns of three words or fewer (26%; 3.5: 47), 24 of them Mas's; 27 turns of 15 words or more (3.5: 25; longest 31, Rima's handover). Mas has 43 turns (median 3 words), 165 words, 12% of the words. 46 turns contain a question. No dashes. Openers: "Okay" is Gerg's (twice, the send-off and the return), "Sorry" is Lunchmas's (once), and Gerg's one "Wait." is in sc 4. One line still opens on a call-out said as its own sentence, the lifeguard's "Sir. Sir.", on purpose: it's how you get a stranger's attention on a beach. Estimated speech plus speaker changes across the episode: ≈ 9.7 min (3.5: ≈ 9.5). Not heard.
- **The conversations, speech and picture reported apart (3.6).** The showrunner's measure is how long people talk, so speech is the first column. "Changes" adds 0.45 s for each change of speaker; "picture" is the written action inside the span (inserts, crosses, holds), a hand estimate kept in the script's `CONV` list. Planned from words at each voice's pace (§2); nothing is timed.

  | Conversation | Turns | Words | Speech | + changes | + picture | Span |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Writing room (sc 18, "It's done, Mario." → "…Publish.") | 20 | 141 | 52 s | 8 s | 10 s | ≈ 70 s |
  | Keycap call (sc 17A, the lifeguard → "caps lock") | 13 | 132 | 46 s | 5 s | 14 s | ≈ 65 s |
  | Keycap call from Gerg's answer | 11 | 108 | 37 s | 5 s | 4 s | ≈ 46 s |
  | Exit interview (sc 11) | 11 | 89 | 36 s | 4 s | 5 s | ≈ 45 s |
  | Alley (sc 2) | 6 | 75 | 28 s | 1 s | 3 s | ≈ 33 s |
  | Send-off (sc 4) | 10 | 83 | 28 s | 3 s | 12 s | ≈ 42 s |
  | Veto (sc 13; 3.5: 22 s of speech) | 8 | 66 | 25 s | 3 s | 14 s | ≈ 42 s |
  | The box, first half (sc 8; 3.5: 18 s) | 6 | 59 | 24 s | 2 s | 1 s | ≈ 28 s |
  | Reunion (sc 21) | 10 | 69 | 24 s | 4 s | 9 s | ≈ 36 s |
  | Nole's step (sc 23) | 7 | 62 | 24 s | 2 s | 2 s | ≈ 27 s |
  | Pledge (sc 14) | 6 | 59 | 22 s | 2 s | 5 s | ≈ 29 s |
  | Ownership (sc 14, 3.5; 3.4: 13 s of speech) | 8 | 53 | 21 s | 3 s | 3 s | ≈ 27 s |
  | Door 1's desk (sc 22) | 5 | 38 | 13 s | 2 s | 2 s | ≈ 17 s |
  | The box, second half (sc 8) | 5 | 23 | 10 s | 1 s | 3 s | ≈ 14 s |

  By speech alone, the writing room (52 s) and the keycap call (46 s) pass 45 s; the exit interview, the alley, the send-off, the veto and the box's first half run 24–36 s. The veto and the send-off carry the most picture inside them (a held clause and a revolving-door gag), so their spans overstate how long people talk. The midpoint's ownership exchange, the thinnest anchor in 3.4, is 21 s of speech in eight turns. The box's first half, a quip ladder in 3.5, is now 24 s in six turns, with the man being pressed given two turns of two sentences (3.6).
- **Orb toasts: 3** (`term sheet`, `human`, `president-elect`).
- **The glass:** one test that someone reads (the stadium's stomp, and Maon reads it); the Woodrose's crystal glass; the glass moved as a chess piece (sc 16–17); the home shot (sc 20).
- **Name cards: 5 freezes** (LUNCHMAS, MAON, MOSWEN, NOTNIH + SIMED as a lineup, SKCAS). LUNCHMAS and MAON now sit ≈ 40 s apart (3.4; ≈ 35 s in 3.3), under the 45-s guide: Maon's card has to land on his first appearance, and the gull and the change of place separate them. SOZEB, KOOC, LEIHT and o1 are plates.
- **Lit UI:** once, about 4 s. **Object labels:** 1 (`PEN`).
- **Boxes:** in-world only (Gerg's video tile, the jumbotron, the monitors). No deliberate `BOX`.
- **The direct confrontation:** Nole, "I'm on the step, Mas. You're in the line.", at the end of an exchange Mas answers four times, and in 3.4 it is Nole's one quiet line of the episode (§6: loud characters get one quiet line, and it lands the blow). **Someone tests Mas's calm (3.4):** Rima's grace (sc 11), AUHSOJ's three pushes (sc 14; 3.5 adds the blank line) and Nole's step (sc 23). **Mas runs the room with questions:** the send-off ("how long?", sc 4), the box in sc 8 ("how long does it get to think?", "and this one?", "that's all of it?"), sc 11, the reunion ("how long?", sc 21, 3.5) and the step in sc 23 ("a while. you?"). **Gerg's tell** (his keys stop): once, in sc 17A. **Looks into the lens: 0** (3.4: the FAN CAM look stays inside the jumbotron's feed, and the clipboard shown to camera is cut). **The small scheme:** the pen (sc 20 → sc 23). **The keepsake:** the keycap (sc 4 → 17A → 21).
- **The secret's hints:** GLYPH through the slats and in the clock; the FAN CAM trying to read him. Nobody says it.

**Guardrails:**
- The election is one fact card (no rally, map or crowd). 3.6 gives the card one small image, the gold podium from behind, and no face; if X11 excludes the card, the icon goes with it.
- RUMPT is props and pose only; his hand is normal proportion and never in close-up.
- **"Say a number." points at BANKSOFT's `$100B`**, never at Mas's check. The check gets only a clink, and the turn follows the pledge, so the picture doesn't assert a trade.
- The `$1M TO THE INAUGURAL FUND` rail stays up while the check goes in, and the memo line reads `for the fund`.
- Mas's place in THE LINE is [INVENTED], and no rail dates him at OGAL-A-RAM: in 3.3 BANKSOFT's `DEC 2024` rail rides only the reflection insert, with Mas out of frame, and clears before the cut back to him (the facts critic found it on his shot in 3.2). "not that worried" plays inside its own clip, on a phone held up by the man in front of him in line, with THE GREY LADY's bug in the clip's corner and no date on screen (3.5; the boom mic is gone, so nothing reads as said on the terrace).
- No yacht, jet or island near OGAL-A-RAM, NOLE or LEIHT. LEIHT is a check and a spinner in a parking lot: no fund office and no second chair (family, §1).
- ROLYAT's Sep 10 beat stays cut (`EXCL §1b`). The exodus is a band breakup, with MARIO and ADELINA as co-founders only. "Directional differences" is an unquoted invented sign. THE OTHER MAS no longer appears: in 3.3 the fuel pump's sticker became `PREVIOUS OWNER: A CRYPTO EXCHANGE` (facts row 25), so no newcomer reads a "MAS" there as the lead, and 3.6 makes it `PREVIOUS INVESTOR: A CRYPTO EXCHANGE`, because "owner" overstated a $500M minority stake. THE WHALE is an unlabelled shadow.
- NOTNIH's lectern is Toronto's, with no ceremony cues.
- **Real lines are in their own medium:** the veto message on its page, Reneiw's statement on his phone, SUCRAM's headline as a post, LUNCHMAS's post on the lobby screen, and (3.5) Mas's interview line in its own clip on a phone. NOTNIH's press conference and Mas's interview line are spoken, because they were spoken. Mas's line is [H], which guardrails §3 keeps to chyrons and headline cards, so voicing it waits on a ruling or an upgrade to [V] (§10 #14). The fallback (3.6) cuts the clip whole rather than playing it mute under a `REPORTED` chyron.
- PP3.1 gets Mas's one small move (the phone face down) and no held face. PLANTAIR carries no rail, no pawn and no military.
- **Dates only where the record has them.** SKCAS's czar card carries no rail; BANKSOFT's pledge rides a month-only rail (`DEC 2024`), so the calendar never runs backward after door 12; the `$1M` rail is undated beside it. The `SAFETY COMMITTEE` lanyard hangs on the hook on Sep 23, a week after the real Sep 16 change (facts row 43), with no rail and no line.
- **No capital "I" on screen before Ep7:** the essay shows its lowercase clause and hedge only; the whole sentence stays in facts.md.
- **The machine's tells stay off hands** (style-range P29): door 3's sleigh drifts on its runners; the six-fingered elf is gone.
- **The V.O. passes [pov §5.5](../../bible/pov-and-framing.md#55-where-vo-is-forbidden):** nothing in the cold open, THE PLAN, an exit, the button or a proceeding, and nothing within a bar of the record. No line claims a feeling, a motive or what he knew.
- **Photosensitivity:** the turbine stays at 2 revolutions per second or less, and its light climbs in palette steps without flashing. The GPU tree cycles one step per beat, and the FAN CAM's circle doesn't flash. All of them go in the luminance audit.
- **Voices:** the notation's voice line (no sound-alikes; RUMPT's final voice is human).
- **The 3.2 lines at real events** stay on money, tech, credit and status, and none gives a reason, a motive or a private term: Rima's "I'm happy to answer anything." (her departure day; she then answers nothing), Reneiw's "That was the whole point of it." and the pen exchange (the veto), the investors' pledge card and bargaining (the reported ask, whose rail followed the joke; 3.6 cuts the rail and labels the list's header `(REPORTED)` instead), Gerg's "awkward timing" beside SUCRAM's real post (a collision, never a reply), "On the benchmarks, anyway." (facts row 5c), SOZEB's "I'd like it noted…" (facts row 37; his place in the line rests on row 37a, still [K], with an unplated-silhouette fallback), Nole's "How long have you been standing there?" / "Me? I sat down." (status on the terrace, never the record), and KOOC's catchphrase. Each is marked `[INVENTED]` and needs the guardrails owner's pass (§10 #11).
- **The 3.4 lines at real events,** for the same pass (all `[INVENTED]`; none gives a reason, a motive, a private term or a cause):
  - Gerg's "A while. It's all in the doc." (his sabbatical day, sc 4): no end date is asserted and no reason given. The real announcement named an end, so the line stays a non-answer rather than a claim.
  - Maon's "You don't put a clock on a kicker, Mas." / "That's for me." (the o1-preview launch, sc 8): about the cartoon stadium; nothing on o1's real thinking time.
  - Rima's handover, "that's not a number." and "No. It isn't." (her departure day, sc 11): logistics only; no reason, no timeline for the decision, nothing near her real farewell wording.
  - Reneiw's plea about the hatchling and Moswen's "It's a very nice pet, Senator." / "I'm signing something." (the veto, sc 13): about the leash, never the bill's contents; no reason for the veto.
  - The investor's "It's very good lemonade." and AUHSOJ's ownership exchange, with Mas's "it's had a good week." and "nobody. that's what makes it one." (the round, sc 14): money and structure only. AUHSOJ is a real person's parody (money only, no relatives, cameos-industry.md). The exchange asserts no term of the round and no cause for it; the round's reported conditions are not used (they aren't in facts.md). "nobody. that's what makes it one." describes a nonprofit (no owners), and the facts owner confirms it needs no row. AUHSOJ signing anyway rests on row 14 (he leads the round, [K]).
  - NOTNIH's `MY STUDENTS` slide (the Toronto press conference, sc 16–17): an invented prop behind a real line; the facts owner confirms it doesn't misstate the event, and the fallback is no slide.
  - Nole's step lines, "Nobody told me there was a line.", "Best seat on the hill.", "There you are." (3.5, was "You can see you.") and the quiet "I'm on the step, Mas. You're in the line." (the terrace, sc 23): status only, never the record.
- **The 3.5 lines at real events,** for the same pass (all `[INVENTED]`; none gives a reason, a motive, a private term or a cause):
  - AUHSOJ's "Remind me who owns it." (was "Who actually owns it?") and his new beat, "Every sheet I've ever signed has a line for the owner. This one's blank." / Mas's "it's supposed to be." (the round, sc 14). The sheet is a cartoon prop, not the round's paperwork, and the lines assert no term of the round; they describe a nonprofit, which has no owner. A real investor's parody at a real round, so it needs this pass. Fallback: cut the two lines; the exchange plays as it did in 3.4.
  - The interviewer's question inside the clip, "Are you worried about Nole's influence?" (sc 23): invented wording, now voiced from a clip of the real interview rather than an off-screen boom. The ruling is whether an invented question may play inside a real interview's medium (open-questions #37). Fallback: the clip carries no voice, only its own caption.
  - Gerg's "how long?" answer, "Twelve days. In a row. Business days." (sc 21), is unchanged; only Mas's question changed ("when?" → "how long?").
  - Mario's "Adelina. That's a sub-concern." (sc 22, o3's announcement): a callback to Ep1's invented line, about his own scroll; nothing on o3's real results.
- **The 3.6 lines at real events,** for the same pass (all `[INVENTED]`; none gives a reason, a motive, a private term or a cause). Where a line above has been reworded, this list gives the wording that now stands:
  - Maon's "The old one kicked before you'd finished asking. You saw where that went." and "You don't put a clock on a kicker, Mas. You put a clock on him, he rushes, and a rushed kick goes through the two." (the o1-preview launch, sc 8): about the cartoon stadium's kickers; still nothing on o1's real thinking time.
  - Mario's "Today it's the R's in a fruit. Next year, who knows what it's counting. I'm drafting something." (the same launch, the rival box): his own worry, as a joke on himself; no claim about any lab.
  - Reneiw's "Governor, before you sign anything, look at him." and Moswen's "It's a very nice pet, Senator. Well trained. Good teeth." (the veto, sc 13): still about the hatchling on the leash, never the bill's contents; no reason for the veto. The naturalness critic asked for this one to go through the pass.
  - Gerg's "I've got caps lock doing enter for now." (sc 17A) and "Caps lock can go back to shouting." (his return, sc 21): about his keyboard. "For now" gives no date for his return.
  - Adelina's "Why on earth not?" (sc 18) and Mario's "Adelina, that's a sub-concern." (sc 22): wording only.
  - Nole's "Best seat in the house. You can see the whole line from up here. Every one of you." (the terrace, sc 23; replaces "Best seat on the hill." and "There you are."): status only, never the record.
  - The interviewer's question inside the clip, now "Nole's suing you, and he's close to the new administration. How worried should you be?" (sc 23; replaces "Are you worried about Nole's influence?"): invented wording, whose two facts are on the record and already on screen (Ep2's suit; Nole on the podium's step in sc 20). It avoids "president-elect", which the Orb's toast saves for the button. The ruling is still open-questions #37's; if the question can't sit inside the real clip, the clip is cut whole.
  - On screen: the list's header `COMPETITORS` [V/K] `(REPORTED)` (sc 14), which now carries the reported ask the cut rail used to; AUHSOJ's plate `AUHSOJ · EVIRHT` / `LEADS THE ROUND (REPORTED)` [K]; the election card's podium icon (sc 20, with X11's ruling).
- **The 3.3 lines at real events,** for the same pass: Gerg's "Same model, just a lot more compute behind it." (Pro's launch, Dec 5; o1 pro mode's "more compute" wording is OpenAI's, to be added to facts row 33); Maon's "The new one just took the test." (o3's Dec 20 announcement with its test scores; "finished" implied a release); Adelina's "Manalt's whole essay fit on one phone screen." (a rival's jab at a real essay's length, not a measurement); the Corkboard Guy's "You know what they're calling the next model? Strawberry." (the reported codename, row 3a); the fuel pump's `PREVIOUS OWNER: A CRYPTO EXCHANGE` (row 25; 3.6: `PREVIOUS INVESTOR`). None gives a reason, a motive or a private term.

**Production, against Draft 2's ≈ 81 agent-h of picture** (Draft 2's pricing notes are retired with this draft; its first-time cast list still stands: RUMPT, SKCAS, SOZEB, MOSWEN, RENEIW, MAON, LUNCHMAS, NOTNIH, SIMED, MIT KOOC, SAMA NOS and the Corkboard Guy, a week ahead of picture):
- **New drawings:**
  - the beach at night (a recolour of the day beach)
  - the investors in the door's wings (the tiled silhouette rig)
  - PLANTAIR's pot (one small prop)
  - the FAN CAM package and the STREAM bug and lower third (code)
  - the strawberry wallpaper still (3.A: a three.js PBR still now, a Blender still for the final)
  - the lobby's ticker strip (one small prop); the pilot's revolving door leaning on the wall, the `Q*` vault door and the `SAFETY COMMITTEE` lanyard are reused from Ep1 and Ep2
  - a few tall busts for the day-room `[MCU]`s: Gerg, Rima, Adelina, Mario, Maon, Moswen, Skcas and Nole
  - **3.4 (the shot review):** three angle plates (the boardroom table from overhead, sc 14; straight down through the ceiling hole, sc 22; up the lighthouse to its window, sc 19; ≈ 15–35 agent-min each); AUHSOJ's close-up (fallback: his exchange plays on the long two-shot, close-up on Mas only); mediums for Reneiw (sc 13), the pledge investor (sc 14), o1 at the tee (sc 8) and Gerg on the night beach (sc 17A); the `MY STUDENTS` slide in Notnih's inset. The saved cuts (two in the FAN CAM, the Orb's clock insert, the clipboard shown to camera, Mas's close-up for "good student.") pay for part of it
  - **3.5 (the three-lens read):** the phone clip in sc 23 (a small in-bezel drawing: a conference stage, two armchairs, the clip's Mas and THE GREY LADY's bug; it replaces the boom mic and its flag); AUHSOJ turning his sheet (a pose on the existing two-shot); the blank tag on Moswen's pen in the wide (a recolour of the tag); Mario's window at door 12 now has Adelina soft in the room behind him (sc 18's set, reused). Saved: the boardroom's door-bang and the courier's wave-out (the pallet rides the lobby wide). Sc 16–17's reorder reuses the same desk-level drawing twice
  - **3.6 (dialogue pass 5):** one new angle plate, the low angle up the dark building to its lit window from Mas's place in line (sc 23, ≈ 15–35 agent-min; the Ep1 owner checks it against Ep1's window); the `NOPEAI` name on the lobby strip and the spire's ticker (a text change on the existing props), scorched blank as the odometer burns in F3.2; the list's `COMPETITORS (REPORTED)` header (sc 14); the podium icon on the election card (sc 20); two plate texts (AUHSOJ, LEIHT) and the sticker's one word (sc 19). Saved: the pledge's second rail
- **Gone:**
  - the vending machine and espresso gags
  - the check silhouette
  - the fingers-crossed scan
  - the portrait windows' open-and-type steps
  - the extra-blocky steps, the shredder card, the paper Rima, the BESTIES blimp and the six-fingered elf (second pass)
- **Net:** about even. The coverage in the ladder's frameless helpers costs no new drawings (pov §4.7.5). **3.2:** slightly cheaper in drawings. The greeter's wide and about a dozen per-line singles go, and the conversations hold on two-shots and over-the-shoulders with mouths and blinks; the added cost is recording (about twice the words) and lip-sync on the held shots.
- **Largest uncertainty (unchanged):** the dialogue-scene rate, which should be measured on Ep1's first real dialogue scene before Ep3 goes to picture.

## 10. Open questions

1. **The podium's order (showrunner):** *clink* → nothing → the `$100B` rises → the turn. It keeps the button and drops the implied trade. The fallback is Draft 2's order (the turn on the clink), which the plan's "nothing links the coin to the turn" argues against.
2. **The door as the turbine:** *resolved by the facts critic (2026-09-26):* the investors ride the door and nothing says why the round happened, so it reads as a rhyme, not a cause.
3. **The naming ruling is still open (naming owner):** `o1`, `o3` and the codename "Strawberry" have no naming.md entry; the facts critic recommends a §3 exception for version strings and codenames (on the precedent of "H20, H200, Blackwell kept as-is"). THE CORKBOARD GUY is plated on screen (sc 2) and needs a §8 line before lock, with the SECOND TRUTHER, as generic fictional composites. *(3.6: the facts review raised both again. The registry line should say he is "not any real account", as his design note does.)*
4. **Still to upgrade before lock:**
   - "i love summer in the garden"
   - the Nov 5 card ([K] → [V])
   - `DEC 5` Pro ([V/K])
   - `FIRST BUDDY` (3.6: no quotation marks now, since it's [K])
   - `PODCASTING BRO`
   - `LEADS THE ROUND`
   - NOTNIH's line (video for [P])
   - the candle's post title: the facts critic gives it as "Elon Musk wanted an OpenAI for-profit" (Dec 13, 2024); pull the post and upgrade from [K]†
   - the Mar-a-Lago visit rows (fallback: undated silhouettes)
   - PP3.1's exact headline words (facts row 41)
   - the committee change (facts row 43) and the Q*/Strawberry report (row 3a)
   - *(3.3)* o1 pro mode's "more compute" (Dec 5) for Gerg's desk line, into facts row 33
   - *(3.3)* the sc 11 newspaper: keep it undated, or match one outlet's exact Sep 25 headline and retag it [H]
   - *(3.3)* row 37's contradictory "[V, SINGLE]" tag, and row 37a's SOZEB visit (fallback: an unplated silhouette with no line)
   - *(3.6)* the list header's `COMPETITORS (REPORTED)` [V/K] (sc 14; facts row 13), which now carries the reported ask the cut rail used to
   - *(3.6)* the facts review repeats two of these as blocking: the Nov 5 card can't air as [K] (an easy upgrade), and "i love summer in the garden" is quoted in the intro before it's upgraded
5. **The `Q*` vault (Act Four owner):** o1 jogs out past the pilot's open vault (sc 8). It depends on the pilot keeping its vault in sc 31; if the Act Four pass cuts it, cut the door here too. *(3.2: Ep1's Act Four draft 5.1 keeps the vault and its `DO NOT OPEN. DO NOT EXPLAIN.` note, so the door stays.)*
6. **The intro window:** SCRIPT §8.1 gives Ep3's window as `Jul–Dec 2024`; this teleplay opens Aug 5. It's the intro owner's call (open-questions #1).
7. **The keycap:** the in-episode keycap is worn blank on purpose, so it never collides with the intro's `MUTE` couch-gag keycap or reads as `RETURN`. Confirm with the intro owner that the two can coexist.
8. **STREAM (style-range pass):** kept, against the continuity critic's note, with the rail dark while it's up (§7).
9. **Carried over from Draft 2:**
   - **F3.1's placement** needs the flashback-map owner's sign-off.
   - **Hill parity** is unsigned (the inkwell).
   - **The ROLYAT handoffs** to the timeline and cameos-world are still open.
   - **The `Give` verb** stays greyed out and unused.
10. **Runtime (showrunner, 3.2; 3.3; 3.4; 3.5; 3.6):** the conversation pass planned the episode at ≈ 20:13 of story, up from 17:27 and inside the 22:00 frame; 3.3 planned ≈ 20:07; 3.4 ≈ 21:39; 3.5 ≈ 21:48; 3.6 plans ≈ 22:06 (see #16). It's an estimate from word counts; the reel's takes will settle it.
11. **Guardrails pass on 3.2's, 3.3's, 3.4's, 3.5's and 3.6's new invented lines at real events** (guardrails owner): the lists are in §9. Legal may want a look at the investors' pledge card, which dramatises a reported ask, and at AUHSOJ's ownership exchange (3.4, extended in 3.5 with the blank owner's line on his sheet), which puts invented words on a real investor's parody at a real round. New in 3.5: the invented interview question now plays inside a clip of the real interview (sc 23). New in 3.6 (§9's 3.6 list): Maon's and Mario's fuller turns at the o1-preview launch, Reneiw's and Moswen's longer turns at the veto (the naturalness critic asked for these to go through the pass), the reworded interview question, Nole's reworded step line, the list's `(REPORTED)` header, and the election card's podium icon.
12. **The stick-figure reel (showrunner note 10):** 3.6 should be read aloud at pace and cut as a stick-figure reel with real takes before any pixel work, so the conversations can be heard and approved. See [open-questions](open-questions.md) #31–#35 for what a human must check and the resources to ask for.
13. **The election card and X11 (guardrails owner, 3.3):** X11's excluded column names "election results", and its "instead" column bans only midterm results. `NOV 5, 2024 · ELECTION. HE WINS.` (sc 20) needs a ruling on which applies ([open-questions](open-questions.md) #3). 3.6 adds one small image to the card (the gold podium from behind, so "HE" has a referent for a newcomer); the ruling covers the icon too.
14. **Voicing [H] words (guardrails owner, 3.3):** "not that worried" (sc 23) is [H] and spoken; guardrails §3 keeps [H] to chyrons and headline cards. Either upgrade it to [V] from the interview's video or transcript, or rule that voicing [H] words is allowed. The same pattern is in Ep1 and Ep2. 3.5 plays the interview as a clip on a phone in the line, in its own medium, with Mas watching himself: if the line is upgraded, it's voiced from the clip; the fallback was the clip with its sound down and the words on a `REPORTED` chyron under it. **3.6 (the insider read):** the fallback is now to cut the clip whole (≈ −8 s), because muted it would carry three words that Nole's exchange then plays out anyway. 3.6 also rewords the invented question ("Nole's suing you, and he's close to the new administration. How worried should you be?") so it doesn't share a rhythm with Ep1's and Ep2's unseen askers ([open-questions](open-questions.md) #37).
15. **Mas's line length (showrunner, 3.3; 3.4):** the showrunner's note says not to cut dialogue to "only a few words per character". Mas's median stays 3 words (his longest invented line, the exit interview's "on a scale of one to five…", is 13), by design: he's the calm, literal centre. 3.4 takes the screenwriter's test for keeping it: his brevity stays only where the counterpart in the scene talks in full, and 3.4 makes that true in every anchor (Rima's handover, AUHSOJ's pushes, Mario and Adelina, Nole's step). Is that the right reading of the note for the lead? ([open-questions](open-questions.md) #39)
16. **Runtime over the frame (showrunner, 3.4; 3.5; 3.6):** story ≈ 22:06 against the 20:45 guide, runtime ≈ 23:21 against 22:00 (3.5: 21:48 and 23:03; 3.4: 21:39 and 22:54). §2's ladder (≈ −51 s, dead picture first, then two whole beats) brings it to ≈ 21:15 without losing a beat, a fact or a real line, and four more small trims that each cost a laugh or a rhyme reach ≈ 21:06. 3.5 took 3.4's rung 2 off the ladder, because Mario's window now carries Ep1's callback; 3.6 rewrites rung 6 so it keeps Moswen's pleasant run, and lists F3.3's pop-up book past the small trims as a whole-beat option (≈ −27 s, to ≈ 20:39; #20). The 3.6 growth (+18 s) is all talk, except Mas's 2-s look up and 1 s on the photo. The recommendation is to hear the reel before cutting anything: every length here is a word-count estimate, and the keycap call's 3.3 plan was ≈ 14 s low. ([open-questions](open-questions.md) #41)
17. **The new angle plates and the lobby plan (production and layout owners, 3.4; 3.6):** the director's review adds three angle plates (the table overhead, sc 14; the high angle down through the ceiling hole, sc 22; the low angle up the lighthouse, sc 19; each ≈ 15–35 agent-min) and a locked lobby floor plan (notation) that the set bible should record. Its fourth plate, a reverse of the lobby from the laptop's camera (sc 11), was not taken (§14). **3.6 adds a fourth plate after all:** the low angle up the dark building to its lit window from Mas's place in line (sc 23), which 3.4 had declined; the newcomer read asked for it so Ep1's two hailstones get an answer a newcomer can see. The Ep1 owner checks it against Ep1's window. ([open-questions](open-questions.md) #42, #52)
18. **Where a successor picks up (lead, 3.5; 3.6):** the Ep3 counting script and its baselines still live only in session scratch (§16, "How to re-count"; the 3.6 copy is in `…/scratchpad/p5fin-ep03/`), and `docs/STATUS.md`, which SHOWRUNNER-NOTES names as the single "start here" file, still doesn't exist. Until the lead copies the counter beside the episode and writes STATUS.md, §16's "Where things stand" is this episode's start-here, and §15 says how to rebuild the counter if scratch is gone. ([open-questions](open-questions.md) #46)
19. **Stale companion files (their owners, 3.5; 3.6):** [outline](outline.md), [beats](beats.md), [gags](gags.md) and [facts](facts.md) still describe Draft 3.1 in places (beats still has "It reads chunks. Not letters.", `THE OTHER MAS`, "I also have a rocket." and `(TENDER)`, though its door-12 "That's a sub-concern." is right again in 3.5; facts rows 13a, 25 and 37 and tables D and E are stale, and 3.5 makes rows 22 and 31 stale too). A successor opening them first will be misled. Each wants a one-line banner ("stale since 3.1; current state is script §16") until its owner re-syncs it. This pass could edit only this script and open-questions. **3.6 makes more facts rows stale:** rows 8 and table B's essay line ("possible that" is back), 13 (the reported ask now labels the list's header, not a rail), 13a (the strip reads `NOPEAI  $86,000,000,000`), 14 (AUHSOJ's plate names EVIRHT), 16 (`BACKS MAS'S FUND`), 25 (`PREVIOUS INVESTOR`), 27 (the podium icon), 28 (`FIRST BUDDY` without quotation marks), and 31 with table B's MAS row (the reworded question; the clip is cut whole if it can't be voiced). ([open-questions](open-questions.md) #47, #53)
20. **F3.3 as a whole-beat trim (showrunner and style-range pass, 3.6):** the insider read calls the pop-up book the first cut after the trim list if the reel plays long, since Ep1's `EX-NOPEAI` plate and sc 12 already say who founded the rival. 3.6 lists it past the small trims (§2, ≈ −27 s) rather than on the ladder, because it costs the episode's one full-frame style leap (3.D), and the showrunner asked for range where it makes sense. Keep it unless the reel is still long after the ladder ([open-questions](open-questions.md) #51).

## 11. Revision log (season revision, 2026-09-26)

Draft 2 → Draft 3. Sources: [season-revision-plan §5](../../production/season-revision-plan.md#5-ep3--ep12_strawberryjpg), [SEASON-NOTES](../../reel/SEASON-NOTES.md) notes 2, 9 and 10, [flow-and-continuity](../../bible/flow-and-continuity.md), [tone-and-dialogue](../../bible/tone-and-dialogue.md), [pov-and-framing](../../bible/pov-and-framing.md) and [SHOWRUNNER-NOTES](../../production/SHOWRUNNER-NOTES.md) as of 2026-09-26.

**Structure**

| Change | Where | Why |
|---|---|---|
| The door spins the odometer: the investors ride it in, and each revolution turns a wheel | Sc 11 → 14 | Note 10: the A-plot is the price; the departures feed it only as an image |
| The stadium is staged from Mas's seat in the box; the cutaways are his eyelines | Sc 6, 8 | Plan §5: Mas share 65% → ≈ 72% at 0 s |

| The podium turns on the `$100B`, not on his clink | Sc 24 | Plan §5, "nothing links the coin to the turn" |
| The Misanthropic run is signposted as the exit, out through his window and back through his glass | Sc 17A → 18, 19 → 20 | POV §6: one exit, ≈ 10% |
| MOSWEN's veto and Reneiw's statement appear on the page and on a phone; SUCRAM's headline is a post | Sc 13, 21 | Tone §5.2 rule 3: real lines in their own medium |

**Beats added**
- **PP3.1** (sc 10, +3.5 s, paid by sc 9): the first unread notification, THE STACK at 1.
- **O3.1** (sc 20, 0 s): PLANTAIR's houseplant.
- **The keycap thread** (sc 4, 17A, 21).
- **Five V.O. lines** (§4).
- **Style moments (proposed)** in sc 8, 15, 19 and 22 (§7).

**Cut**

**Dialogue work**
- **Mas acts, not echoes.** "a return.", "it's on the list.", "where people leave." and "it is now." stay. Readbacks go. He asks the questions at the door.
- **Gerg** is in sentence case with new lines drawn from the episode's own tech: "If it breaks, don't call. I'll know." / "Nothing's broken. I'd know." / "Then it's enter." / "I remapped caps lock."
- **The Corkboard Guy** gets his one quiet line ("I know.") and one caps word per scene.
- **Name cards** land as toppers during an action (LUNCHMAS mid-step).
- **Rails** stamp the landing, never the top of a home room.

**Grammar**
- The portrait windows are gone: frameless close-ups, over-the-shoulders, two-shots, desk-level and frame-in-frame shots instead, with boxes only where the world has them.
- Music is spotted per sequence as continuous cues, with written stops only on story beats: the shutter, the clock, the pen, the lounge at the reflection.
- Holds carry room tone. The old "beats are cut in as silence on the grid" rule is retired (flow §7).

**Net runtime:** ≈ −1:24 (§2).

- **Dropping STREAM** (continuity #5): kept, because the calendar is the stream's own UI and the rail goes dark under it, so it replaces a layer instead of stacking one. The style-range pass decides.
- **Cutting the Corkboard Guy's "twenty seconds" opener** (insider #3): kept. It turns the figure into a character laugh ("I've never thought about anything for twenty seconds."), and it's the third of three tellings, each a different beat.
- **Cutting SOZEB's plate** (insider #7): kept as drama #11's `SOZEB · NOZAMA`, the third way: one short plate that tells a newcomer who he is and ties him to the van in sc 20.

The plate takes the newcomer's role line instead.

**Net runtime:** ≈ −1:15 against Draft 3 (§2). Nothing was added that needed its own time.

## 12. Revision log (conversation pass, 2026-09-26)

Draft 3.1 → 3.2, by the Ep3 dialogue writer. Written to the showrunner's note on Ep1's Act Four v4 (below), [SHOWRUNNER-NOTES](../../production/SHOWRUNNER-NOTES.md) notes 10 and 11, [flow-and-continuity §4](../../bible/flow-and-continuity.md#4-dialogue-rhythm), [tone-and-dialogue](../../bible/tone-and-dialogue.md) R17–R21 and the §6 rhythms, the fifteen rules in [dialogue-craft §4](../../_sources/research/dialogue-craft.md#4-fifteen-rules-for-mr-mas-dialogue), and Ep1's Act Four (drafts 5.0 and 5.1) as the model of the voice. **Nothing in 3.2 has been read aloud, recorded, heard or watched.** Every length is planned from words (§2), and every count below is taken from the script text.

> **Showrunner, 2026-09-26:** "it is getting cloesr. however, a lot of the dialoge is unnatural and the cuts are still quite fast. a few time dialogue seems to randomly blurt out or cut off. it seems since v2 you didn't allow any conversation to play out for more than a few seconds which makes it hard to follow. i said to cut down empty time, but not to cut every dialogue into only a few words per character"

**What the note meant here.** The note was given on Ep1's Act Four, and the lead found the same shape in this script. Measured on 3.1:
- **Counts:** 139 voiced turns and 632 words, a median of 4 words a turn, and 64 turns of three words or fewer (46%).
- **Turn length:** outside the P.A. announcer, no invented turn ran past 19 words.
- **Ladders:** most scenes were question-and-quip ladders, cut to a new single on nearly every line: "Title?" / "*Machines of Loving Grace.*" / "Great on a tote bag."; "What do I get for two hundred?" / "It thinks harder." / "Than what?" / "Than a PhD."; "you dropped a key." / "I've dropped eleven. Which one?"
- **No interruptions:** 3.1 had no dashes, so its problem wasn't cut-offs. It was talk clipped to a few words a person, and lines that landed cold: "Mine's fine." answered nobody, "I'll return this." came with no trigger, and Maon's intercom voice arrived without a name.
- **The causes, as in Act Four:** the word caps (Gerg 6), the old R8, and a tightening pass that took story from 18:42 to 17:27 partly by trimming talk.

**What 3.2 does.**
- **Conversations that play out.** Each talk scene now has an ask, a push-back and a turn, in complete thoughts. The longest (all planned, not timed):
  - the writing room: Adelina wants it published, Mario wants one more caveat, and "That's the pitch deck." turns it (≈ 65 s)
  - the keycap call: Gerg told him not to call, and he calls (≈ 50 s with the lifeguard)
  - the exit interview: Rima promises to answer anything, then answers nothing (≈ 40 s)
  - the pledge: an investor reads the card out, and another bargains for the lemonade stand (≈ 35 s)
  - the alley: the Corkboard Guy against a Second Truther who pushes back (≈ 30 s)
  - the box: Maon explains why you let it think, then defends the sticky note (≈ 12 s and ≈ 17 s)
  - the rival box, the reunion, SHIPMAS's desk and Nole on the step (≈ 12–20 s each)
- **The counts** (3.1 → 3.2):
  - 139 → 154 voiced turns and 632 → 1,203 words.
  - The median turn goes from 4 to 6 words, and to 7.5 for invented turns by anyone but Mas.
  - Turns of three words or fewer go from 64 (46%) to 46 (30%). 27 of the 46 are Mas's; the rest are kept punchlines and buttons.
  - 24 turns now run 15 words or more (3.1: 3); the longest is Mario's letter-counting memo at 28.
  - Mas has 41 turns (median 3 words) and 13% of the words. He stays the shortest speaker in every room, complete and lowercase, and his short lines now land against people who talk in full.
- **Somebody talks to Mas, and he answers:** Gerg (sc 4, 12, 17A, 21), Rima (sc 11), the investors (sc 14), Maon (sc 8, 22), Nole and Kooc (sc 23). He asks the questions in sc 8 and sc 11.
- **No blurts.** Every new voice has a lead-in, and every line has a trigger and a listener:
  - the Second Truther's raised hand
  - the ring before Gerg answers in sc 17A
  - Gerg asking Mas about the Woodrose, and Mas's "and yours?" before "Mine's fine."
  - Reneiw noticing the pen's tag before "I'll return this."
  - the intercom's crackle and "Mas, it's Maon."
  - the cook's "you're blocking my window" before the fling
  - Mario's line said to Adelina
- **Holding coverage.** Each conversation is carried by one held setup that cuts on turns: the exit interview's two-shot; the writing room's two-shot; the box's two-shot (replacing the frameless 50/50 and four singles); the pledge's over-the-shoulder; the send-off's two-shot; Nole on the step in one over-the-shoulder; the veto's over-the-shoulder. About a dozen per-line singles are gone.
- **Paid for with empty picture:**
  - the Misanthropic greeter's wide (sc 4; the badge reader carries the same joke)
  - the reasoning machine's `MORE THINKING → BETTER ANSWERS` label (sc 7; Maon says it)
  - those per-line cuts
  
  The rest of the time is new, and it is talk. Ep3 was 3:18 under the 20:45 frame, so the pass spends that headroom on conversation, not on anything else (§2).

**Scene by scene.**

| Sc | What changed | Why |
|---|---|---|
| 2 | The Second Truther's doubt becomes a real push-back ("I don't want to be that guy. But I zoomed out a little."); the Corkboard Guy doubles down ("So nothing red is just red anymore."); the cook gets a second sentence, which gives the fling its reason | An exchange with a turn, not a riff and a feeder; no "picture", so the intro's reveal stays unspent |
| 4 | Gerg's send-off in whole sentences (the doc, the pin, the alert); "Oh, and if it breaks, don't call. I'll know."; "Wait. Is that LUNCHMAS? Out on the bridge?"; "Not even close. Footnotes are load-bearing over there."; the greeter's wide cut; one held two-shot | Gerg talks while he types; Mas's two lines answer him; the plant is kept word for word inside a fuller line |
| 7 | Maon's three lines become three whole turns in his dry register; step 1 gains a bar; the dial label goes | A voiced PLAN in speech, told once; no label repeats the voice (the Ep1 blueprint's rule) |

That's how you get two." / "Let it think… Longer."; the note gets "that's all of it?" / "That's the part you get to see." before "for my safety?" | "Let it think." and "Mostly the competitors'." now land at the end of conversations; Mario gets his roast and a second laugh |

I'm happy to answer anything." before the two "Thank you all."s; "and you two? names, for the form."; Gerg's "I can only see Rima from here."; the installer's "I just need a signature for the door."; the exit interview holds on one two-shot | The clip joke gets a promise to break; Mas runs the room with questions; nobody gives a reason (SEASON-NOTES note 10) |

Who actually owns it?"; one held over-the-shoulder | A negotiation with a stake, so "which one?", "it's on the list." and "the nonprofit." are moves |
| 15 | "that one had confetti." is said to the waiting table | A listener |
| 17A | The call plays out: "I've got an alert on every service and an alert on the alerts." / "i know. nothing's broken." / "Mas, is this a social call?" / "you dropped a key. in the lobby, in august." / "I've dropped eleven since August…" / "Then it's enter. It's the one I hit the most." / "Sure. Hang on to it." / "I remapped caps lock."; the lifeguard and Gerg get second sentences | The episode's quiet two-hander, now long enough to be one; still only about a key, never about reasons or his return |
| 18 | The writing room is one argument in a held two-shot: "It's done, Mario. … Publish it." / "It's nearly done. … Some of the footnotes have footnotes…" / "Manalt's fit on a phone." / "Then he left things out." / "It ends well. It's actually hopeful." / "Don't tell anyone." / "What are we calling it?" … Lunchmas: "They said to follow the paper."; "Are we the geniuses?"; "…Publish." is now the turn | Every 3.1 laugh is kept, and the scene gains a shape: the ask, the push-back, the turn. "The footnotes have footnotes." (the old restore list's second item) is back |
| 21 | Gerg's return in whole sentences ("three months of notifications"); "There it is. I've been hitting caps lock since August." pays sc 17A and releases the V.O.; "Is that a new collar? You had three when I left."; "Well. That's awkward timing. We're shipping twelve things."; one held two-shot | The reunion plays; "awkward timing" makes SUCRAM's post a collision rather than a cause |
| 22 | The desk: "Two hundred dollars a month. What do I actually get…?" / "…it goes away and really works on it before it answers." / "Harder than what?" / "Than a PhD. On the benchmarks, anyway." (its phrase grows to about 8 bars); ChatGTP's "Ninety-nine percent! You're nearly there!"; "Three, six, nine, twelve."; Mario's line to Adelina; "Mas, it's Maon. The new one's finished." | Each door's joke gets its run-up inside the swing; the benchmark clause keeps the PhD claim accurate |
| 23 | The silhouette CEO and SOZEB fuller; Nole's confrontation becomes an exchange ("How long have you been standing there?" / "a while." / "I didn't stand. I'm on the step. You're in the line." / "it's a good line."), held on one over-the-shoulder; KOOC's "We're not first. We're best." | The direct confrontation plays as a conversation Mas answers twice; KOOC's catchphrase gives the after-you routine a character laugh |
| All | Every invented voiced line is now marked `[INVENTED]` | The firm script rule; 3.1 used "no tag means invented" |

**Kept short on purpose.** Buttons and punchlines whose shortness is the joke, each now with a longer line in front of it:
- "it's a strawberry.", "Never used it.", "roughly.", "good student.", "Check.", "Partially.", "Concerning."
- "Thank you all." ×2, "I'm aware.", "leaders.", "it is now.", "a return.", "it's on the list.", "the nonprofit."
- "…I have a PhD.", "I know.", "We summarized it.", "Say a number."
- the five V.O. lines, unchanged
- the Woodrose roll call

**Declined, and why.**
- **Reading the record aloud** (R21 allows it). The essay's line, SUCRAM's headline, LUNCHMAS's post, the veto message and Reneiw's statement are written sources, and they stay in their own medium (tone-and-dialogue §5.2 rule 3). Nobody in Ep3 reads a real line aloud; the talk goes round them. Lunchmas asks "Is that us?" of a line we read on the scroll.
- **A longer farewell for Rima.** It would drift toward her real post's wording or toward a reason. Her fuller line is a promise ("I'm happy to answer anything.") that her clip then breaks.
- **An argument about SB 1047's substance** in Moswen's office. It would put invented reasoning on a real veto. The exchange stays on the light touch and the pen.
- **Nole answering "not that worried".** His lines stay on status (the step, the line) and never touch the record.
- **A speaking elf at door 3, and any new speaking character.** The elf's look is the trigger for "it's a preview.".
- **Overlaps or cut-offs for texture.** No scene here is heated enough to earn one.

**Real lines.** All unchanged, word for word, with the same tags and in the same media. The one addition is a tag: Mario speaking the essay's title is marked `[P · the essay's title]`.

**Guardrails and POV.**
- Invented talk stays on money, tech, credit and status. No line gives a reason for a departure, a motive at a real event, a private term, or a causal link between two real events. The list for the guardrails owner is in §9.
- Mas states no feeling and no motive.
- The Woodrose stays Gerg's memory in his rim. Mas's "and yours?" is heard on the call in the present, not V.O.
- The exit (sc 18–19) grows to about 10% of the story.

**Handoffs (nothing outside this file and [open-questions](open-questions.md) was edited).**
- **The gags owner:** [gags.md](gags.md) still has 3.1's wording for the lines changed above (sc 2, 4, 7, 8, 9, 11, 12, 13, 14, 17A, 18, 21, 22, 23).
- **The facts owner:** the new texture that leans on a row is tagged in the script: rows 5, 5a, 5b and 5c (Maon; "On the benchmarks"), 19 (the word count), 1 and 28a ("three months of notifications", with the sabbatical ending in November), 33 (the twelve days) and 37 (SOZEB). Nothing new needs a fetch.
- **The recording supervisor:**
  - All 154 turns are to be recorded fresh by the Act Four v5 method ([voice-diagnosis-v4 §4](../ep01/production/act4/voice-diagnosis-v4.md)): whole turns in one read, at the character's pace, with no time-stretch.
  - Rima's "Thank you all." is recorded once and used twice.
  - About 40 turns contain a question. The yes/no ones ("Could it be… a fruit?", "Is that us?", "Is that renewable?", "Is this a social call?", "that's all of it?", "for my safety?") need a rise that stock voices may not give.
- **The composer:** the cues now duck and thin under longer talk. THE PLAN's step 1 is 4 bars, and SHIPMAS's desk phrase is about 8.
- **The reel editor:** re-time the V.O. gaps from the new takes (§5).
- **The beats and outline owners:** no beat was added, cut or moved.

**What a human must check** (I can't listen or watch):
1. Read the episode aloud at pace with a timer. Does it sound like people talking, not a trailer and not a memo? Do the planned lengths hold?
2. The radio test on sc 11, 17A and 18. With eyes closed, is it clear who is talking to whom, and what each wants?
3. Does the stick-figure reel keep the energy of the set-pieces, the stadium and SHIPMAS, now that more talk runs inside them?
4. Question intonation in the temp takes.
5. Fresh newcomer and insider reads of 3.2, against §3's must-understand list and the insider's drag list.
6. The guardrails pass on §9's list of 3.2 lines, and legal on the pledge card.

**Resources that would speed this up** (showrunner note 10; asked through the lead):
- a human read-aloud or table read
- a TTS with better question intonation (designed voices only, never a clone)
- a real-time watch of the stick-figure reel
- web access for the [K] items in §10 #4

**Net runtime:** ≈ +2:46 of planned story, to ≈ 20:13 (§2).

## 13. Revision log (naturalness pass, 2026-09-26)

Draft 3.2 → 3.3, by the Ep3 dialogue writer (second pass). Sources: three page reads of 3.2 — the dialogue-naturalness critique (N below), the newcomer and insider critique (NI) and the facts and guardrails review (F) — with [SHOWRUNNER-NOTES](../../production/SHOWRUNNER-NOTES.md) and [flow-and-continuity §4](../../bible/flow-and-continuity.md#4-dialogue-rhythm) as of 2026-09-26. **Nothing in 3.3 has been read aloud, recorded, heard or watched.** Lengths are planned from words (§2); counts come from this pass's own counting script, run on the script text.

**What the critiques found here.** The conversation pass worked, and the critics kept the alley, the send-off, the owner's box, the exit interview, the pledge, the keycap call, the writing room's argument and Kooc's "after you". What was left was five patterns, mostly one line each:
- everyone opened the same way ("Okay…" five times, "Sorry…" four times, Gerg's "Wait." twice)
- a fact told twice (Gerg re-explaining "thinks harder" after Maon's PLAN; "twenty seconds" twice in two alley lines; the tour-bus horn before F3.3's bus)
- replies to questions nobody asked ("I didn't stand.", "…So, the door.", "leaders.", "and yours?")
- a joke repeated from Ep2 (ChatGTP's "Great question! Honestly, one of my favorites.")
- lines that are unclear by ear ("Manalt's fit on a phone.", "how long does it get?")

**Counts (3.2 → 3.3, both by this pass's script, which reads 3.2 as 155 turns and 1,204 words where 3.2's own count said 154 and 1,203):**
- 155 → 154 voiced turns and 1,204 → 1,187 words. The median stays 6 words a turn; 8 for invented turns by anyone but Mas (7.5 in 3.2).
- Turns of three words or fewer: 47 → 44 (29%), 23 of them Mas's. Turns of 15 words or more: 24 → 21 (longest 28, Mario's memo).
- Mas: 41 turns, median 3 words, 153 → 158 words (13%).
- Openers: "Okay" 5 → 2 (both Gerg's), "Sorry" 4 → 1 (Lunchmas's), Gerg's "Wait." 2 → 1.
- The longest conversations (planned): the writing room ≈ 64 s (≈ 49 s of speech), the keycap call ≈ 50 s from the ring to "I remapped caps lock." (≈ 45 s of speech, so its 0:50 plan is tight), the exit interview ≈ 40 s, the pledge ≈ 35 s, the alley ≈ 30 s.

**Declined, adapted or handed off, and why.**
- **Upgrading "not that worried" to [V]** (F #27): I couldn't open the interview in this session (its web-search budget was used up). The line stays spoken, with the ruling asked and a chyron fallback written into the scene (§10 #14).
- **The X11 ruling** (F #26) isn't mine to make; the card is flagged (§10 #13) and unchanged.
- **Mario's "That was the executive summary."** stays, as the naturalness critique advised; Ep2's "That's the summary." is the one to change (the Ep2 owner).
- **`PODCASTING BRO`** (NI newcomer, low) stays as a small stamp. The big `RETURN TO SENDER` carries the pallet for anyone who missed Ep2's rail, so missing the stamp costs nothing.
- **SAMA NOS's plate** stays off (held back for Ep4; the critic agrees).
- **Gerg's two "Okay"s** both stay. The openers note gives "Okay" to Gerg, and sc 4's send-off and sc 21's return bookend each other.
- **"Same model, more compute, as long as it needs."** is adapted to "Same model, just a lot more compute behind it.": "as long as it needs" claims more than o1 pro mode's "uses more compute to think harder". The compute fact goes to the facts owner for row 33, since row 5c (the critic's citation) covers only the PhD benchmarks.
- **"I go through about one a month."** is adapted to "I wear out about one a month.", so it can't be heard as more of the eleven he's dropped.
- **Cutting the lifeguard's lines instead of moving them** (NI insider #5's other option): declined. The move keeps the socket gag (the season's plug in miniature) and puts the pier Gerg just blacked out behind "Nothing's broken. I'd know.", which is the scene's biggest laugh.
- **"Is that renewable?" / "it is now."** (NI insider #7) came with no fix; "What's that running on?" / "the door." is mine: a plain question, a literal answer, and the turbine said aloud once.
- **Nole's line "reads as written"** (NI insider #7): "Me? I sat down." now answers Mas's "you?" out loud, and the confrontation keeps its kernel, "I'm on the step. You're in the line."
- **"Manalt's whole essay fit on one phone screen."** is taken as written and tagged as Adelina's jab, not a measurement: the real essay is short, but no phone shows it on one screen.
- **The Mas-line-length question** (NI across-episodes #4) goes to the showrunner (§10 #15).

**Handoffs (nothing outside this file and [open-questions](open-questions.md) was edited).**
- **Gags owner:** [gags.md](gags.md) now lags two passes (sc 2, 8, 9, 11, 12, 17A, 18, 22 and 23 changed again in 3.3).
- **Facts owner:** row 33 (o1 pro mode's "more compute"); row 9 (the sc 11 newspaper: undated, or one outlet's exact headline as [H]); rows 37 and 37a (the contradictory "[V, SINGLE]" tag, and SOZEB's visit); row 25 (the sticker's new wording); §D's list of invented lines, which should take 3.2's and 3.3's.
- **Naming owner:** naming.md lists THE OTHER MAS in Ep3 as "3 (sticker)"; 3.3 takes his name off it, so Ep3 drops from his row.
- **Guardrails owner:** X11 and the election card (§10 #13); voicing [H] words (§10 #14); the 3.3 lines at real events (§9).
- **Ep1 owner:** confirm that sc 23's lit window above the terrace matches the pilot's OGAL-A-RAM window and flash 7's silhouette. Ep1's handoff line "Ep3 has already cut its recall" is now true: "That's a sub-concern." is gone.
- **Ep2 owner:** change Ep2's "That's the summary." (sc 18); Ep3's candle (`NOLE WANTED A NOPEAI FOR-PROFIT`) only lands as irony if Ep2 makes clear that the suit claims a promised nonprofit.
- **Recording supervisor:** 45 turns contain a question; the new yes/no ones include "Want me to count them again?" and "a while. you?".

**What a human must check** (I can't listen or watch):
1. Read the episode aloud at pace with a timer, especially every line changed here.
2. The eyes-closed radio test on sc 11, 12 and 23, which the critique named, and on the reordered sc 17A.
3. In sc 17A the phone now rings for about 12 s while Gerg deals with the lifeguard. Does it play as Gerg happily ignoring the ring, or as a stall?
4. Whether sc 23's lit window reads at all at the terrace wide's size, and whether a newcomer links it to the pilot.
5. Question intonation in the takes.
6. Fresh newcomer and insider reads of 3.3, by readers who haven't read 3.2.
7. The guardrails and facts passes on §9's two lists.

**Net runtime:** ≈ −0:06 of planned story, to ≈ 20:07 (§2).

## 14. Revision log (scene-craft pass, 2026-09-26)

Draft 3.3 → 3.4, by the Ep3 writer (the scene-craft pass). **Nothing in 3.4 has been read aloud, recorded, heard or watched.** Lengths are planned from words (§2); counts come from the 3.4 counting script (below). Only this file and [open-questions](open-questions.md) were edited.

> **Showrunner, 2026-09-26:** "also for the dialogue, one thing to point out that was part of issue earlier is each conversation was reduced to minimum lines to get a point across without feeling like real dialogue that helps the viewer feel the scene. let's make sure generally we are using generally good filmography and storywriting trends"
>
> And, on documentation: "also as far as documentation, we want to be sure that we are leaving appropriate detail where someone could pick up where we left off."

**Sources.** The note above; [SHOWRUNNER-NOTES](../../production/SHOWRUNNER-NOTES.md) notes 8 and 11 (let conversations play out; cut empty time, not talk) as of 2026-09-26; [tone-and-dialogue](../../bible/tone-and-dialogue.md): "Scene craft and film grammar", R1–R21 and §6; the [dialogue-craft reference](../../_sources/research/dialogue-craft.md); [flow-and-continuity](../../bible/flow-and-continuity.md) §4 and §5a; [pov-and-framing](../../bible/pov-and-framing.md) §2.3 and §4.7. Two reviews of 3.3, both page reads that edited nothing: a **screenwriter's scene-craft critique** (S below, nine findings) and a **director and editor's shot review** (D, scene by scene plus eight patterns). Neither review is filed in the repo; everything taken from them, and everything declined, is recorded here so nobody needs them.

**What the note meant here.** 3.2 and 3.3 had already made the lines complete sentences (3.3 by the 3.4 count: 148 turns, 1,165 words, median 6; the lead's own count was ≈ 1,060, and 3.3's script counted 1,187: three parsers, so use one). The problem the reviews found was how the scenes were built:
- The four scenes that should be felt most (the exit interview, the midpoint, the writing room's turn, the line) ran as relays of gags, and in each the person pressing Mas, or being pressed, barely talked (Rima 15 words; the midpoint's key question from "INVESTOR 4"; AUHSOJ, who leads the round, silent; Nole's confrontation four lines).
- Mas's want (a number) lived only in pictures; nobody refused him a number to his face, and nobody tested his calm.
- Several exchanges read the picture back, and several must-read picture beats had no shot, or were drawn too small inside wides.

**What 3.4 does.**
- **Each rebuilt scene carries a short italic "Want, obstacle, turn, cost (3.4)" note** under its slug: who wants what, the tactic in the way, the turn, the price. A successor should keep those notes true when changing the scene.
- **The middle goes back in.** An opening move, a push-back, a change of tactics, then the turn (sc 4, 8, 11, 13, 14, 17A, 18, 21, 23).
- **The want reaches conversation** as a runner, never as a statement (notation; §1): refused by Gerg, Maon, Rima, the clock, AUHSOJ and Maon again; given only by Gerg, back ("Twelve days."); turned on him by Nole ("How long…?" / "a while.") and RUMPT ("Say a number.").
- **The counterpart talks in full** wherever Mas is short: Rima's handover (31 words, the episode's longest turn), the investor's pledge and bargaining, AUHSOJ's two pushes, Nole's performance, Mario's reason.
- **Film grammar:** establish then move in; tighter as it escalates; close-ups on the turns; coverage that holds; reaction shots where the listener is the story; the 180 line (sc 8, 23); stepped cut-ins, screen pushes and drifts instead of zooms (sc 6, 13, 17A); sound bridges at 4→5, 17A→18 and 19→20; a locked lobby floor plan with screen direction as meaning (notation).
- **Paid for with dead picture** where there was any: the door flurry (−4 s), the clipboard shown to camera (sc 11), the form beat ("MAS writes something… We don't see what.", which nothing paid off), the `MAR 2012` card, the Orb's clock insert (folded into a two-plane frame), two FAN CAM cuts, and Rima's silent revolution (her answer now carries it). **And with talk that only read the picture back, or stalled a scene, or had no listener** (R7, R18, R19), none of it a real exchange: "two question marks.", "What's that running on?" / "the door.", "Excuse me. What's that?" / "a return.", "Every floor is the alignment floor." and "I also have a rocket.". No conversation was shortened to make room. The rest is new time (§2).

**Declined or adapted, and why.**
- **Rima's handover (S #1), adapted.** The sketch's "one name on every system, and the on-call rotation runs through March" is replaced by "Every system in it has a name next to it now, and none of them are mine.": a rotation planned through March implies a long-planned departure, which is a claim about the record's timeline. "Tuesday's review" stays as texture.
- **The `$7T` pallet (S #2 said cut it), kept with a wave-out.** It carries beat #12, facts row 10's `PODCASTING BRO` stamp and a Money-camp roast; the director's other option (wave it out) removes the feeder exchange that read its label back and keeps all three.
- **AUHSOJ's wording (S #2), adapted.** "Before anybody signs." → "Before I sign." (the investor has just signed his pledge card); "A nonprofit worth a hundred and fifty-seven billion dollars." → "A nonprofit. At a hundred and fifty-seven billion." (the round values the company the nonprofit controls, not the nonprofit itself).
- **Nole's step (S #3), adapted.** "First day." (asserts when Nole arrived) and "They bring you things." (a perk claim) are dropped as too close to the record; SOZEB keeps his plate and his first line, which has a listener (the podium) and rests on facts row 37, and loses only the line to no one.
- **Mario's reason (S #5), adapted.** "Because we're the ones who worry…" states his card and the theme (R6); the line is recast in the footnote runner, and a push-back and its hurt ("Nobody reads the footnotes now." / "I read the footnotes.") are added so the middle has a turn of its own.
- **The veto's plea (S #6), adapted.** "I made it as light as a bill can be. There's almost nothing left in it to veto." claims the bill was gutted, which is substance; the plea is about the hatchling on the leash, and "light" is saved for the pun.
- **"the whole company fit at one table" (S #7), adapted** to "everybody": the Woodrose dinner came before the company existed.
- **Cutting sc 9 (S #8), declined.** It carries beat 7a and it's talk; the 0-s sharpening is taken, and the cut is ladder rung 8 if the reel plays long.
- **No cards line in the box (S #9), respected, with a coach's line added to the first half instead.** The warning was about naming "competitive advantage" twice; "You don't put a clock on a kicker." and "That's for me." name neither, and set up "That's the part you get to see."
- **Lunchmas's distant "Sorry!" in sc 4 (S #9), declined.** He's on the bridge across the Bay, behind the lobby glass; nobody would hear it, and sc 18's "Sorry. Hi." is his opener.
- **Scenes the screenwriter said not to expand (2, 7, 22, 24, the windows):** not expanded. Their changes here are shots only, plus sc 22's folded flurry.
- **The lobby reverse from the laptop's camera for the `? ?` reveal (D sc 11), declined.** It's a new lobby plate; the gag is now told once, by the writing insert, and Gerg's new line doesn't need the reveal. Adapted: D's "cut the writing insert" became "cut the clipboard shown to camera and the line", which also removes the lens look.
- **The low angle up the building for the lit window (D sc 23), declined for now.** Nothing in Ep3 needs the window to land; it's marked an egg, and the Ep1 owner decides (§10 #17, open-questions #42).
- **Kooc's two-shot (D sc 23), adapted** to a tight wide: he has no medium rig in this episode.
- **Dropping the figure from F3.2's rail (D sc 15)** is the facts owner's call; unchanged.
- **More talk in the reunion (D "for the writers")**: taken lightly (two lines). A longer catch-up would have to name what Gerg missed (Rima, the round, the election), which Ep3 keeps light by design.

**Counts (3.3 → 3.4, both by the 3.4 script):**
- 148 → 171 voiced turns and 1,165 → 1,344 words (+179, +15%). The median stays 6 words a turn; invented turns by anyone but Mas: 8 → 7 (more short replies inside longer exchanges).
- Turns of three words or fewer: 43 (29%) → 47 (27%), 24 of them Mas's. Turns of 15 words or more: 21 → 25 (longest 31, Rima's handover).
- Mas: 36 → 42 turns, 132 → 160 words (11% → 12%), median 3.
- The anchor scenes (turns / words, 3.3 → 3.4): sc 11 18/115 → 18/140; sc 14 9/74 → 12/93; sc 18 17/114 → 20/148; sc 23 12/79 → 15/105; sc 17A 11/119 → 13/128; sc 13 4/32 → 8/59; sc 8 15/147 → 19/166; sc 4 8/72 → 10/81; sc 21 8/58 → 10/68.
- The longest conversations (planned): the writing room ≈ 72 s (3.3 ≈ 59 s), the keycap call ≈ 63 s from the lifeguard (≈ 59 s), the exit interview ≈ 45 s (≈ 25 s), the send-off ≈ 42 s (≈ 38 s), the veto ≈ 39 s (3.3's four lines ran ≈ 13 s), the reunion ≈ 35 s (≈ 30 s), Nole's step ≈ 27 s (≈ 14 s), the pledge ≈ 29 s and the ownership exchange ≈ 18 s.
- Estimated speech plus speaker changes across the episode: ≈ 8.0 → ≈ 9.3 minutes.

**How to re-count** (3.4's tool, kept for the record; **§15 has the current copy, the exact commands and the rebuild spec**):
- `python3 /tmp/claude-1000/-home-jgon-project-art-mrmas/a5e7723c-6ab4-4824-a1ed-8e367fdb82e5/scratchpad/craft-ep03-writer/count.py` prints the totals, a per-scene table (turns, words, median, longest, turns of three words or fewer, speakers, estimated talk seconds) and the conversation spans; add scene ids (`… count.py 11 14 23`) to list their turns; `--file <path>` counts another draft (3.3 is saved beside it as `script-3.3-before.md`, with the 3.3 output in `baseline-3.3.txt`).
- The parser is the Ep3 critic's (it counts every `**NAME**` block above "# Writer's notes", strips parentheticals and tags, and leaves out `MAS (V.O.)`); it reads 3.3 lower than 3.3's own script did (1,165 vs 1,187), so compare drafts only with the same parser. The talk seconds use the voice guide's paces and 0.45 s a change of speaker; the conversation spans add a hand estimate of the picture inside each (the `CONV` list in the script).
- The scratchpad is session-local. **If it's gone,** the method above is enough to rebuild it: split the script at "# Writer's notes", take each bold speaker block until the next blank line, strip `` `[…]` ``, `[…]` and `*(…)*`, and count `[A-Za-z0-9$'’]+` runs.

## 15. Revision log (three-lens pass, 2026-09-26)

*(3.6: [§16](#16-revision-log-dialogue-pass-5-2026-09-26) is now the start-here. This section stays as the record of 3.5, and its "How to re-count" spec and file table still hold.)*

Draft 3.4 → 3.5, by the Ep3 writer (the final pass on the showrunner's "scenes to be felt" note). **Nothing in 3.5 has been read aloud, recorded, heard or watched.** Lengths are planned from words (§2); counts come from the Ep3 counting script (below), the same parser as 3.4's. Only this file and [open-questions](open-questions.md) were edited.

> **Showrunner, 2026-09-26:** "also for the dialogue, one thing to point out that was part of issue earlier is each conversation was reduced to minimum lines to get a point across without feeling like real dialogue that helps the viewer feel the scene. let's make sure generally we are using generally good filmography and storywriting trends"
>
> "it seems since v2 you didn't allow any conversation to play out for more than a few seconds which makes it hard to follow. i said to cut down empty time, but not to cut every dialogue into only a few words per character"
>
> "also as far as documentation, we want to be sure that we are leaving appropriate detail where someone could pick up where we left off"

### Where things stand (start here)

- **The episode.** Draft 3.5 is a page draft, planned at **≈ 21:48 of story and ≈ 23:03 with the intro and credits** (≈ 1:03 over the 20:45 guide; §2 has the ladder). It has never been heard. The next step is the one [SHOWRUNNER-NOTES](../../production/SHOWRUNNER-NOTES.md) sets ("Stick figures first"): read it aloud at pace, record whole turns, and cut a stick-figure reel so the showrunner can hear the conversations before any pixel work (§10 #12).
- **What's measured and what isn't.** Measured, from the text only: turns, words, lengths per line, and speech time estimated at each voice's pace (below and §9). Not measured: anything heard or watched. Every "plays", "reads" and "lands" in these notes is a page judgement, and the reel replaces it.
- **Which files to trust.** This script is authoritative. [open-questions](open-questions.md) #41–#50 are the live questions. [outline](outline.md), [beats](beats.md), [gags](gags.md) and [facts](facts.md) are behind it (they still describe Draft 3.1 in places; §10 #19 lists what's stale), so where they disagree, this script wins. [flashbacks](flashbacks.md) and [intro-slot](intro-slot.md) weren't checked in this pass.
- **Where the reasoning is.** Each rebuilt scene carries an italic "Want, obstacle, turn, cost" note under its slug; keep those true when changing a scene. §14 is the full record of how the scenes were rebuilt in 3.4, and this section records what 3.5 changed on top of it.
- **What's open, by owner:**
  - **Showrunner:** the runtime (§10 #16, open-questions #41), Mas's brevity (#39), the podium's order (#2).
  - **Guardrails owner:** §9's lists of invented lines at real events (3.2 to 3.5), especially AUHSOJ's exchange; the invented question inside the real interview clip and voicing [H] words (#37); the election card against X11 (#3).
  - **Facts owner:** rows 22 and 31 (changed in 3.5), plus 1, 9, 33, 37, 37a and 43 (#36, #38, #43, #49).
  - **Ep1 owner:** Ep1 open question 51 is now answered: Ep3 keeps "That's a sub-concern." (#48).
  - **Lead:** move the counter into the repo and write `docs/STATUS.md` (#46); put banners on the stale companion files if their owners can't re-sync them soon (#47).

### Source

One review of 3.4: a **three-lens page read** (a newcomer retell, an insider read and a read for feel) that edited nothing and ran the 3.4 counter unchanged. It isn't filed in the repo, so everything taken from it, and everything declined, is recorded here. Its verdict, in short: the through-line holds for a newcomer without real-world knowledge; the anchors now play as scenes (sc 11, 12, 17A, 18 and 21 were felt); the midpoint's ownership exchange was the thinnest anchor (≈ 13 s of speech, played as a syllogism); three jokes were told before they landed; five lines rang false; and the counter lived only in session scratch while the companion files had gone stale. Priorities below are the reader's (P1 first).

### Declined, adapted or handed off, and why

- **Sc 4, adapted.** The reader's optional "Is that who I think it is?" is a stock phrase (R20). "Is that one of ours?" also keeps the name off the line, and hints at who's leaving.
- **Sc 9's POV note: no change.** It's the public chorus, and it's already ladder rung 7 if the POV owner objects.
- **Sc 12, adapted.** The reader proposed `(UDIAB)` alone as fine print. Facts row 22 dramatises Amodei's Baidu-to-2016 timeline on this card, so `JOINS 2016` stays too, as fine print (the facts owner confirms, #49).
- **Sc 14, the round's reported condition** (convert to for-profit within two years), which the reader offered as an alternative to the blank line: not used, because it isn't in facts.md. It's handed to the facts owner as an option (#49).
- **Sc 15, LEIHT's comeback check** (the reader's optional note that it dilutes the sadness): kept. It plants his fund for Ep6 and Ep10, his plate is the newcomer's reason he's there, and it's the smoke that carries the cut back to the present.
- **Sc 15, the `$43.4M` told twice** (the burnt odometer, then the rail): the facts owner's call, as in 3.4. Unchanged.
- **Sc 18's opener, adapted.** The reader cut the opener to "One of the footnotes still needs a caveat." That loses "Some of the footnotes have footnotes", the scene's first laugh and a restore from 3.2, so 3.5 drops only "It has footnotes." (nine spoken uses → six, where the reader's version reached five).
- **Strawberry is never tied to o1 by name** (a minor newcomer gap): no change. The strawberry on the tee, "Send in the new one." and the `o1-PREVIEW` plate make it inferable, and a spoken tie would be the kind of explanation §5a warns against.
- **Mas's brevity (#39) and "that's not a number.":** kept, as the reader advised.
- **The documentation items for the lead (D1, D2, D6)** are outside the two files this pass could edit: moving the counter into the repo, banners on outline, beats, gags and facts, and creating `docs/STATUS.md`. They're handed off with exact commands (below; open-questions #46–#47). Until then, this section is the start-here, and the rebuild spec below makes the counter reproducible if scratch is lost.
- **D5, the sub-concern, decided: restored.** Ep1 asked the Ep3 owner to keep or cut it (Ep1 open question 51). Keeping it gives Ep1's held-back word its payoff (plant, then one callback), gives the door-12 window shot a reason to stay, and costs about 2 s inside a shot that was already running. Its listener is named and in the room (R19). The Ep1 owner only needs to mark question 51 settled.
